# LOOM Website UI/UX Element Kit

**Campaign:** ui-ux-website-elements

**Concept:** Buttons, toggles, cards, navigation, modals and other interface components rendered in the woven-thread brand language, for use as design-system reference art on the LOOM site.

---

## 1. ui-ux-website-elements-000 (4:5, scene 0)

Primary call-to-action button component sheet: five states (default, hover, active/pressed, focus-ring, disabled) of a pill-shaped button stitched from glowing magenta yarn with visible knit texture, label 'Start weaving'. soft magenta and cyan rim lighting from two sides, deep shadow fill. straight-on eye-level composition. a few loose glowing thread strands drifting across the frame. LOOM brand palette: deep near-black violet background (#0d0716), signature magenta (#f21c8c) and violet (#7b2fbe) as primary glow colors, cyan (#59e6ff) as secondary accent, warm gold (#ffc740) used sparingly for highlights, clean off-white (#f2f0f7) for any light surfaces. High-end 3D animated feature-film character-art style, original in-house design. Do not reference or imitate any real animation studio, franchise, or trademarked character. Do not depict any real company logos or trademarks. LOOM branding must be clearly present in this scene: work a clean, crisply legible 'LOOM' wordmark (all capital letters, spelled exactly L-O-O-M, simple modern sans-serif) into the background somewhere natural to the scene — as signage, a hanging banner, a woven label, a screen graphic, packaging text, or embossed lettering. The wordmark must render as real, correctly spelled, sharp text, not decorative squiggles or fake glyphs. Every product, bottle, package or storefront shown must be explicitly branded as a LOOM product (e.g. its label reads 'LOOM' plus a short product line name), never a generic or unnamed item. No photo reference required — this is original UI concept art. Render as a clean design-system poster/sheet. Do not include any text other than the short functional labels specified in the scene description plus the LOOM wordmark where called for.
